Growth in other advanced economies has also been
more resilient than expected, prompting upward

revisions to near-term forecasts. AI-related investment
and stronger underlying momentum have supported
activity in the United States, while defence spending and
broader resilience across services and investment have
contributed to growth in the euro area. Activity in the
United Kingdom has also held up better than expected,
supported by business investment. These tailwinds
across advanced economies have so far offset some of
the drag to activity from higher energy costs. However,

external forecasters and central banks expect
persistently elevated energy costs to reduce household
purchasing power, raise firms’ costs and increasingly
weigh on consumption and investment. In some
advanced economies, tighter financial conditions will
also weigh on growth.
3.2 The global outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 66

### Page 70

The outlook for GDP growth in China is broadly
unchanged from the August Statement, though
there have been some changes to the expected
timing and composition of growth. GDP growth is
forecast to be 4.6 per cent in 2026, before easing slightly
to 4.5 per cent in 2027 and 4.4 per cent in 2028. The
pick-up in growth in the September quarter is expected
to continue in the near term, given the announcement
of new policy support for infrastructure investment and

signals from authorities that supporting economic
activity will be elevated as a near-term policy priority.
Infrastructure investment is now expected to recover
from late 2026 as this support takes effect, while
structural headwinds are expected to continue weighing
on domestic demand more broadly. The timing and
effectiveness of the policy support remain uncertain,
particularly given high local government debt and a
range of barriers to some local governments

commencing infrastructure projects.
Longer-term government bond yields have risen
across advanced economies, alongside resilient
activity and persistent inflation. The increase appears
to reflect a combination of higher expected near-term
policy rates and higher longer-term real rates, reflecting
a mix of higher term premia and potentially higher
neutral rates. Market-based measures of long-term
inflation expectations remain consistent with central

bank targets. Strong investment and public demand
may be placing upward pressure on real rates, and
expectations of stronger future productivity growth –
including from the adoption of AI – may also be
contributing. Higher public and private debt issuance
and uncertainty about inflation and fiscal trajectories
may also be adding to the compensation investors
require to hold longer-duration bonds (see Box A: How
Does an Increase in Longer-term Real Yields Affect

Financial Conditions? and Box B: Developments in the
Neutral Rate of Interest).
The global inflation outlook has been revised higher,
reflecting an increase in energy prices and
continued resilience in global demand. Consensus
forecasters expect headline inflation to remain above
central bank targets into 2027 in many advanced
economies. Services disinflation has stalled, while core
goods inflation has picked up. The outlook for globally
traded goods inflation has been revised higher,

reflecting higher commodity prices and further strong
increases in export prices for semiconductors and other
AI-related goods across parts of Asia. AI-related price
pressures have not broadened materially beyond
electronics but could become more persistent or spread
to other parts of global supply chains if strong demand
continues to run ahead of the expansion in productive
capacity (see Key risk #2). The further increase in energy
prices since August could add to these upstream cost

pressures, although the extent and timing of
pass-through to consumer prices remains uncertain.
Overall, we continue to judge that the risks to global
inflation remain skewed to the upside.

### Page 71

Australian GDP growth is expected
to remain below our estimate of
potential growth throughout the
forecast period.
GDP growth has slowed since the end of 2025 and is
expected to remain subdued over the next couple of
years (Graph 3.3). The expected slowing in growth over
2026 reflects the effects of several headwinds weighing
on private activity, including the reduction in household
disposable incomes from higher inflation, weaker
conditions in the established housing market, and the

tightening in monetary policy, which more than offset
very strong data centre-related investment. Growth is
expected to remain below potential over the forecast
period, reflecting an assumption that financial
conditions remain restrictive (see Key judgement #2).
Consistent with the latest state and federal budgets,
public demand is expected to continue supporting
growth over the forecast period.
The risks to activity appear to be broadly balanced.

Stronger-than-expected global growth and further
strength in AI and data centre-related activity pose
upside risks to domestic growth (see Key risk #2).
However, historically low consumer and business
sentiment could be pointing to the risk that the
combined effect on domestic activity of the Middle East
conflict, the decline in housing prices and the tightening
in monetary policy could be more than assumed (see
Key risk #3).
The level of GDP at end-2028 is expected to be

broadly unchanged from the August forecasts.
Growth is projected to be slightly stronger than
expected in the near term, reflecting a much stronger
outlook for data centre-related investment compared
with the August Statement. Weighing against that is the
higher assumed market path for the cash rate, a weaker
outlook for the housing market and higher oil prices.
These pull growth in the latter part of the forecast period
a little below that expected in the August Statement.

Business investment is expected to increase strongly
and the outlook has been revised up significantly,

largely driven by data centre-related investment.
Business investment was stronger than expected in the
June quarter and firms’ surveyed expectations of future
investment were again revised higher. External
projections for the expected increase in Australia’s data
centre capacity through to 2030 have also continued to
be revised up as the size of the development pipeline
has increased. Our forecast for data centre investment is
in line with recent baseline external projections, but

below projections that assume Australia becomes a
significant regional hub for data centres. There is
considerable uncertainty around the extent of data
centre investment over coming years. This will depend
on a range of factors such as government policy,
community expectations, future demand for AI services
and grid capacity. We continue to expect that much of
this additional investment will be imported (around
70 per cent), limiting the boost to GDP growth. However,

data centre construction, as well as any potential
spillovers to higher investment in energy and utilities
infrastructure, will require domestically sourced labour
and materials. At this stage, most of this investment
pipeline is in New South Wales and Victoria where liaison
information suggests capacity constraints in the
construction sector are somewhat less binding.
3.3 Domestic outlook

### Page 72

At the same time, a higher assumed cash rate path, a
more significant easing in the established housing
market and higher global oil prices are expected to
weigh more on activity than in the August forecasts.
The forecasts are conditioned on a higher market path
for interest rates relative to August. In addition,
established housing prices have continued to fall more
quickly than expected, with external forecasters now
expecting a deeper downturn than assumed a few

months ago. The outlook for oil prices is also much
higher than assumed in August, which will weigh on real
household incomes. Reflecting these factors, the outlook
for household consumption and dwelling investment
has been revised lower, as has the outlook for business
investment unrelated to data centres.
The labour market is expected to
continue easing over the forecast
period, consistent with
below-potential GDP growth.
The unemployment rate is forecast to increase to

5.1 per cent by the end of 2028, which is around
1⁄4 percentage point higher than previously
expected (Graph 3.4). The unemployment rate has risen
by more than expected over recent months, and the
upward revision to forecast relative to the August
Statement largely reflects this higher starting point.
Elevated cost-of-living pressures appear to have
encouraged workers to enter or remain in the labour
force to a greater extent in recent months than was

expected at the time of the August Statement. These
pressures are expected to continue to support
participation for some time. The participation rate is
expected to decline very modestly by the end of 2028 as
easing cyclical pressures are expected to more than
offset the longer-run upward trend in female
participation. A slightly weaker outlook for demand later
in the forecast period is also contributing a little to the
upward revision to the unemployment rate profile by

late 2028.

As in previous forecasts, the
employment-to-population ratio is expected to
decline through to late 2028. Employment growth is

expected to remain broadly stable over the next year, in
line with the leading indicators of labour demand. From
mid-2027, employment growth is expected to be a little
weaker than in the August Statement, reflecting the
weaker outlook for GDP growth later in the forecast
period. The forecast employment-to-population ratio is
similar to the August Statement in the near term, before
declining more quickly to reach a lower level by the end
of the forecast period.

The economy is expected to move
into balance in 2027, with spare
capacity then emerging in the
economy over the rest of the forecast
period.
Below-potential output growth and an easing in
labour market conditions are expected to see the
economy move from a position of excess demand to
around balance in 2027, before some spare capacity
emerges. The timing of the return to balance is slightly
later than in the August Statement, as a result of the
upgrade to near-term GDP growth.

However, there is considerable uncertainty around
the assessment of when the economy will return to
balance and the extent of spare capacity that
emerges. The impact of conflict-related costs on
inflation over the period ahead will also make it more
difficult to assess the degree of underlying capacity
pressures in the domestic economy. Our assumption for
potential output growth over the next couple of years
assumes productivity growth will pick up gradually (see

Key risk #4). However, with the level of labour
productivity remaining around the same rate as prior to
the pandemic – implying near-zero productivity growth
on average over that period – continued weak
productivity outcomes may be constraining the ability of
firms to grow their supply capacity without generating
inflationary pressure.
Wages growth is expected to pick up
a little in the near term, then
moderate gradually from 2027 as
labour market conditions ease.

The forecast for year-ended wages growth has been
revised a little higher across the forecast period. In
the near term, nominal wages growth is expected to be
supported by the effect of higher-than-expected
capacity pressures in the first half of the year as well as
the Fair Work Commission’s award wage decision (which
had already been incorporated in the August forecasts).
The influence of both higher-than-expected inflation
outcomes and inflation expectations on wage

negotiations is expected to add further upward pressure
on wages growth relative to the August forecast. Some
large wage increases in enterprise bargaining
agreements are expected to support wages growth in
the near term, although the timing of pay increases for
these agreements may contribute to volatility in
quarterly outcomes, particularly for the public sector.
Wages growth expectations from firms in the RBA’s
liaison program are elevated and suggest little

easing over the year ahead (see Box C: Insights from
Liaison). This is consistent with the central forecast for
wages growth. Growth is expected to moderate from
mid-2027 onwards, reflecting easing labour market
conditions and lower inflation outcomes. Wages growth
is expected to have eased to 2.9 per cent in year-ended
terms by end-2028 (Graph 3.5).

Unit labour cost growth is forecast to
be elevated in the near term, before
easing over the second half of the
forecast period.
Year-ended growth in unit labour costs (ULCs) is
expected to increase in coming quarters, reflecting
weak productivity growth and higher wages growth.
Year-ended growth in ULCs is then expected to
moderate from late 2027 onwards, alongside slowing
nominal wages growth and the pick-up in productivity
growth to its assumed medium-term trend growth rate

of 0.7 per cent per year (Graph 3.6). Nominal ULCs are
the measure of labour costs most relevant for firms’ cost
of production and so for inflation outcomes. Year-ended
ULCs growth is expected to remain above 2.5 per cent –
the rate that is consistent with inflation being
sustainably at target – until late in the forecast period.

The forecast for underlying inflation
has been revised higher over the
next year, mainly owing to higher
conflict-related cost pressures and

Trimmed mean inflation is expected to remain
above 3 per cent until the end of 2027, easing
gradually to reach 21⁄2 per cent around mid-2028
(Graph 3.7). Year-ended underlying inflation is expected

to remain elevated at a little above 31⁄2 per cent until
mid-2027, reflecting existing capacity pressures in the
economy and the pass-through of higher
conflict-related costs to consumer prices. Elevated
inflation expectations are also contributing to
underlying inflation over the coming year or so. From
mid-2027 onwards, inflation is expected to ease due to
the emergence of spare capacity in the economy and
labour market and as conflict-related cost effects begin

to unwind.

### Page 75

Cost pressures related to the Middle East conflict are
expected to place more upward pressure on
underlying inflation over the year ahead than
previously assumed. A possible re-escalation in the
conflict had been flagged as an upside risk to inflation in
the August Statement. Since then, oil and fuel prices
have increased by much more than had been assumed
and are now assumed to remain at a higher level over
the forecast period. We expect higher global energy

prices will contribute to higher domestic inflationary
pressures in late 2026 and early 2027, even as domestic
activity slows and the labour market eases (Graph 3.8).
The judgement around the degree of pass-through from
global energy prices to domestic prices is broadly similar
to what had been assumed in August. It is consistent
with information from liaison and the Business Outlook
Scenario Survey (BOSS) in September, where firms
reported plans to continue raising prices in response to

past cost increases arising partially from the conflict.
From mid-2027 onwards, we expect cost effects to
unwind in net terms, albeit to a lesser extent than
assumed in the August Statement as oil prices are now
assumed to remain at a higher level than previously
expected and refined margins are assumed to remain
elevated for longer (see Key judgement #1). The balance
of risks to conflict-related inflationary pressures remains
skewed to the upside, particularly as crude oil and

refined fuel prices could remain elevated or rise further
(see Key risk #1).
Relative to the August forecasts, domestic capacity
pressures are also expected to exert a bit more
upward pressure on inflation over the year ahead.
We now judge that stronger-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures explain more of the recent
unexpected strength in underlying inflation (see
Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). Those higher capacity
pressures are also expected to contribute a little more to

inflation over the year ahead. This upward pressure is
expected to ease gradually over the forecast period, as
spare capacity emerges in the economy and the
labour market.

Elevated inflation expectations are expected to

contribute to inflationary pressure over the forecast
period, supporting the pass-through of firms’ costs
to consumer prices. Several measures of short- and
medium-term inflation expectations have increased
alongside the recent increases in fuel prices, and some
measures of longer-term expectations have also drifted
higher (see Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). We judge
that elevated expectations will contribute to higher
realised inflation by supporting expected cost growth,

making firms more willing to pass these higher costs
through to prices, and placing upward pressure on
wages growth. If the conflict in the Middle East is more
protracted than assumed in our baseline forecasts, there
is a risk that inflation expectations may increase further
(see Key risk #1).

Headline inflation has been revised
higher in the near term due to
stronger growth in fuel prices and
higher underlying inflation.
Headline inflation is expected to remain above 3 per
cent until late 2027, which is around three quarters
longer than in the August forecast, and reach the
midpoint of the 2–3 per cent inflation target range a
bit later than expected in August (Graph 3.9). Headline
inflation is expected to peak at 4.5 per cent in the
December quarter this year due to

higher-than-expected fuel prices. The assumed decline
in global oil prices is expected to put downward
pressure on headline inflation from early 2027. Over the
remainder of the forecast period, headline inflation has
been revised higher relative to our August forecasts,
reflecting higher underlying inflation (Graph 3.10).
Headline inflation is expected to reach the midpoint of
the target in mid-2028 as capacity pressures and
conflict-related costs ease.

### Page 77

The staff assess that the risks to the domestic inflation
outlook are skewed to the upside, despite becoming
more balanced since the August Statement as some of
the risks identified then have been incorporated into the
central forecast. In August, we identified three main
sources of upside risk to the global and domestic
inflation outlook: a re-escalation of the Middle East
conflict; domestic capacity pressures being greater than
assumed; and the global AI boom generating more of an

inflationary impetus than assumed. Since then, the risk
of a re-escalation of the Middle East conflict has
crystallised and staff have re-evaluated the extent of
domestic capacity constraints; both of which have led to
upward revisions to the central inflation forecast over the
next year. However, there is still uncertainty around the
outlook for global energy prices, the role of inflation
expectations in price and wage dynamics as well as the
impact on inflation from AI activity.

The staff also identified in August that there were upside
risks to global and domestic economic activity from
AI-related demand, but that domestic economic activity
could be weaker if the Middle East conflict and the
decline in housing prices affected the economy by more
than assumed in the central forecast. These remain key
risks to the outlook.
Key risk #1 – High inflation could be
more persistent if energy prices
remain elevated, or if inflation

expectations increase or start to
have a greater bearing on wage- and
price-setting.
We have modelled a scenario where crude oil
remains around US$100 per barrel and refined
product prices remain at elevated levels over the
forecast period. Many external forecasters are revising
higher their outlook for crude oil and refined product
prices, on concerns that some of the key margins of
adjustment in global energy markets that have helped
to cushion the impact of the disruption to oil supply and

global activity are reaching their limits (see Chapter 2:
Economic Conditions and Key judgement #1).
Persistently higher global oil prices would leave
domestic inflation higher for a longer period than in
the central forecast. Higher oil prices would directly
increase fuel prices and firms’ input costs, with the latter
gradually passed through to consumer prices as firms
adjust to persistently higher costs. By contrast, the
central forecast assumes a decline in oil prices, which

starts to ease cost pressures related to the Middle East
conflict from mid-2027, contributing to lower inflation
over time (Graph 3.11). Higher inflation also raises
inflation expectations in the scenario, reinforcing
inflationary pressures over the forecast period. Assuming
that the cash rate and exchange rate follow the same
path that underpins the central forecasts, underlying
inflation would be around 23⁄4 per cent by
end-2028 rather than being around the 21⁄2 per cent.

3.4 Key risks to the outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 74

### Page 78

It is possible that inflation expectations could rise
even further or that inflation expectations provide
greater impetus to wages growth and inflation
through second-round effects. The central forecasts
and scenario assume that the increase in households’
and firms’ inflation expectations do exert some upward
pressure on inflation and wages growth. However, given
households and firms are paying particularly close
attention to inflation, it is possible that inflation

expectations will influence price- and wage-setting
decisions by more than assumed. This heightened
attentiveness to inflation could also make expectations
more persistent and more responsive to realised
inflation. With headline inflation forecast to rise further in
the near term, this increases the risk that inflation
expectations remain higher for longer than assumed in
our central forecast.
Key risk #2 – The global AI demand
boom may continue to drive up

global growth and inflation.
MTP growth may not moderate as expected over the
next few years if AI-demand growth continues at the
same pace. It is possible that growth forecasts could
continue to be upwardly revised by Consensus
forecasters if the current upswing in AI-related
investment (and spillovers associated with the boom) is
more persistent than currently assumed. The rise in
interest rates across the yield curve in many advanced
economies may reflect higher neutral rates – that is,

expectations that demand will prove more resilient and
inflation more persistent than currently assumed. Higher
longer-term real rates may partly reflect expectations of
stronger future productivity growth, including from the
adoption of AI. Further investment in anticipation of
higher productivity could also support demand and add
to capacity pressures over the next few years, while most
of the productivity gains are likely to manifest beyond
the next two years (at least in Australia).

The ramp-up of investment in data centres may also
contribute to capacity and pricing pressures
domestically, particularly by drawing on resources in
the construction industry. These price pressures could
be stronger than currently expected as the forecast
pipeline of investment is worked through, or if
investment proves stronger than expected.
Key risk #3 – Domestic economic
activity could be weaker than
expected.
It is possible that the headwinds affecting

households and businesses over the next year could
have a larger adverse effect than assumed. The
central forecasts assume that higher oil prices, higher
interest rates and lower housing prices all weigh on
domestic economic activity broadly in line with average
historical relationships. Timely indicators of spending
remain consistent with this assumption, with no
evidence of a material slowing in consumption growth
in recent months. However, the very weak levels of

consumer and business sentiment could be pointing to
a more pronounced slowing in household and business
spending growth than assumed.
We have calibrated a downside domestic activity
scenario where there is a larger response in
household and business spending to these
headwinds. There is a range of estimates for the effects
of these headwinds in the literature, highlighting the
uncertainty around their impact on economic activity.
Drawing on this evidence, the scenario uses larger

responses than assumed in the central forecast, thereby
identifying a plausible downside outlook for domestic
activity. In the scenario, firms feel forced to pass on the
conflict-related costs to prices as in the central forecast;
but households respond more cautiously to the
resultant pressures on real income and wealth, reducing
discretionary consumption by more than assumed in the
central forecast. Such an effect on spending might be
compounded by a tightening in borrowing constraints

and incentives for housing investors. The scenario
assumes firms respond to weaker demand and margin
pressure with a sharper pullback on (non-data centre)
investment and hiring. Overall, the level of GDP is around
1.0 per cent lower than the central forecasts
by end-2028.
The larger pullback in household and business
spending results in weaker activity and employment
growth, but only modestly lower inflation than in
the central forecast, assuming interest rates and the

exchange rate follow the same path as the central
forecast. The greater caution exhibited by households
pushes the gross household saving ratio up gradually to
a peak of around 17 per cent, where it remains over the
remainder of the forecast period. The greater reduction
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook

in hiring pushes the unemployment rate 0.3 percentage
points higher by end-2028, creating more spare capacity
in the domestic economy than in the central forecast
(Graph 3.12). The additional spare capacity in the
economy and the labour market reduces inflationary
pressures somewhat, but the assumption of cost
pass-through by firms limits this effect. Overall, trimmed
mean inflation reaches 21⁄2 per cent only one quarter
earlier than in the central forecast.

Key risk #4 – There remains
considerable uncertainty about the
degree of capacity pressures in the
domestic economy and labour
market.
Despite the recent reassessment, it is possible that

we are still understating the extent of capacity
pressures. While we assess that capacity constraints in
the economy and labour market have been tighter than
previously judged, model-based estimates of the
unemployment rate that is consistent with low and
stable inflation continue to point to greater tightness in
labour market conditions than our central assessment. In
addition, if productivity growth were to remain subdued
over the forecast period, rather than pick up modestly as

assumed, it may suggest that the economy’s supply
capacity would be lower than currently expected. The
implications for inflation would depend on how
households and businesses adjust their spending
and expectations.
Conversely, capacity pressures may be weaker than
expected. The broader suite of full employment
indicators points to somewhat less labour market
tightness than our central assessment. It is also possible
that more of the recent strength in wages growth and

inflation reflects factors other than excess demand, such
as conflict-related cost effects or higher inflation
expectations. In these cases, wages growth and inflation
may moderate more quickly than in the central forecast.
Productivity growth could also recover more quickly
than assumed, including if the anticipated benefits of AI
adoption and other technological developments are
realised sooner.
There is also a risk that the unemployment rate
increases by more than expected if labour supply

remains stronger than assumed. There is a risk that
elevated cost-of-living pressures could support labour
force participation for longer, or to a greater extent, than
assumed. If so, stronger labour supply could result in a
higher unemployment rate than forecast, even if labour
demand evolves broadly as expected.

1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1780 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1740 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1680 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 385: FX Daily_ A dollar downturn lacks the rate ingredient

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ A dollar downturn lacks the rate ingredient.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The post-midterm dollar correction requires a worsening of USD
rate diﬀerentials to turn into a downturn. But as oil prices fall,
other central banks are facing similar or even larger dovish
repricing than the Fed. Data is key now, but indications are that
they won’t turn USD-negative soon. Today, expect a hike in the
UK and rate holds in Norway and Mexico

Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Chris Turner
We don't see much room for the dollar drop to extend
USD: Front-end rates should prevent a
bigger decline
The Democratic sweep at the midterms delivered the negative
dollar impact we had expected. But as we argued before the vote,
we didn’t see a case for a bigger USD downturn. In our view, rates
need to start moving against the dollar to justify a greater
adjustment, unless idiosyncratic US asset underperformance fuels
the move. But fears that a Democratic sweep would hit tech stocks
didn’t materialise. Instead, a potential relief that the midterm risk

### Page 2

event is out of the way, alongside plummeting oil prices, is fuelling
an equity rally.
The key hurdle for a bigger USD downturn is that lower oil prices are
not unequivocally USD-negative. Let’s look at yesterday’s moves in
rates. As crude dropped 8%, two-year USD swap rates fell 5bp,
broadly in line with CAD, AUD and SEK swaps. EUR, GBP and NZD
2yr swap rates, however, fell more, by around 8-10bp. To us, that is
the most compelling argument against chasing the dollar much

lower. Poorer US data and/or a more dovish Fed remain the
necessary conditions for front-end USD rates to fall more than
peers, and by extension for a broader USD downturn.
Instead, data has remained broadly supportive of Fed tightening
expectations. Yesterday, the ISM services index was a tad softer
than expected but remains well into expansionary territory. The
employment subindex rose to 51.4, the highest since February.
Paired with strong ADP payrolls at 86k, that’s likely to reinforce

expectations for a robust payroll print tomorrow – consensus is 65k,
but the whisper number has risen to 70k.
Today’s data highlights are the Challenger jobs cut ﬁgures for
October and 3Q unit labour costs. We favour a stabilisation around
101.0-101.50 in DXY in the coming days, rather than another break
lower.
Francesco Pesole
Elsewhere, Banxico announces its rate decision later today. The
peso has been the worst-performing EM currency over the last two

months after Banxico said it would not be following the Fed with a
rate hike in September. In fact, some are looking for Banxico to heap
more fuel on the peso’s ﬁre with a rate cut today. Such a move
could see USD/MXN push to 19. Either way, Banxico has slashed the
peso’s rate protection to ultra-low levels, and the peso remains very
vulnerable to global shocks.
Chris Turner
EUR: Knot pitches his hawkish agenda

### Page 3

Klaas Knot delivered a few remarks yesterday after the Netherlands
oﬃcially backed his candidacy for the ECB presidency. He was
ﬁrmly hawkish, claiming second-round inﬂation eﬀects will
probably pick up, and that the ECB will need to respond with higher
rates to small shocks that will lift inﬂation. We’ll wait to hear from
Pablo Hernández de Cos, who is the other frontrunner for the job,
and a long-standing dove. An appointment of Knot would be a

EUR-positive event given his hawkish views, in our view.
Meanwhile, eurozone inﬂation accelerated from 3.8% to 4.0% as
expected in October. That brings the ECB closer to a December hike,
as argued here by our macro team, even if core inﬂation remained
anchored at 2.5%.
As mentioned in the USD section, the EUR:USD two-year swap rate
gap widened against the euro yesterday as oil prices tumbled. A
similar dynamic prevented EUR/USD from rallying in the summer

when oil fell back to $70. Now, at least one more ECB hike – likely in
December – looks like a done deal, providing a stronger ﬂoor for
eurozone rate expectations. Still, this rate component, combined
with lingering French risk, leaves us unexcited about a break higher
in EUR/USD. We continue to see little upside potential beyond
1.130, our year-end target.
Francesco Pesole
GBP: BoE to resume tightening today
We expect the Bank of England to hike rates today by 25bp to 4.0%

(full preview here), in line with market expectations and consensus.
A roughly 25% rise in household energy bills in January now looks
increasingly likely, and we expect headline inﬂation to peak just
above 4% early next year. That has often been indicated as the
pain threshold for the BoE. Still, evidence of second-round
inﬂationary eﬀects remains limited.
We expect a 7-2 vote for a hike, and don’t see the BoE committing
to further tightening at this stage. A follow-up move in February

remains our base case, but policymakers should keep the
emphasis ﬁrmly on incoming evidence of energy pass-through into

### Page 4

wages and prices. An explicit signal that further hikes are likely
would therefore constitute a hawkish surprise.
We think today’s hike can validate the pound’s recent gains, for
now. However, a February hike is fully priced in, and the bar for a
hawkish surprise lifting the pound seems fairly high. We don’t see
immediate rebound potential for EUR/GBP, which is still dampened
by the French eﬀect, but GBP/USD is facing a less supportive short-
term rate diﬀerential, as the GBP curve shows greater dovish

ﬂexibility when oil prices drop. We currently expect stabilisation
around 0.840 in EUR/GBP, while Cable may see its upside capped at
1.350 in the near term.
Francesco Pesole
NOK: Norges Bank on hold today
Norges Bank should keep rates on hold today. Having been one of
the quickest central banks to respond to fresh inﬂationary risks and
given rates are already at an elevated 4.5%, the bar for further
tightening remains fairly high. Incidentally, September CPI ﬁgures

were a bit softer than expected, with underlying inﬂation staying at
3.0% and headline accelerating less than expected to 3.4%.
These are still elevated ﬁgures, so don’t expect Norges Bank to close
the door to rate hikes yet. The threat of further action, if needed, will
remain for some time, but we do not expect any further tightening
in Norway, in line with Norges Bank’s September rate projections.
Markets are pricing in just under 50% chances of another hike by

March.
The krone is facing some headwinds due to the big drop in oil
prices. However, it’s protected by much less hawkish pricing than
other central banks, therefore facing less dovish pressure on energy
price corrections. Yesterday, the NOK two-year swap rate dropped
only 2bp. While this oil move can take EUR/NOK back to 10.50, a
return to $80-85/bll may be needed for a return to the late
September 10.90 levels. We see the pair ending the year in the

10.55-10.65 area.

### Page 5

In Sweden, following yesterday’s 25bp rate hike, inﬂation ﬁgures for
October came in well below consensus. CPIF slowed more than
expected to 1.1%, with the core measure excluding energy
plummeting to 0.1% YoY from 0.5%. These were preliminary
ﬁgures, and expectations are anyway of a pickup to above 2.0% in
both CPIF and the ex-energy measure in 2027, in our view, justifying
another hike in February. That likely explains the contained SEK
reaction to the print. Still, these ﬁgures support our view of a

consolidation around 11.00 in EUR/SEK as opposed to another big
leg lower.
Francesco Pesole
CEE: Lower energy prices overshadow
NBP and CNB meetings
In Poland, the NBP left interest rates unchanged at 3.75%
yesterday, as expected, with the accompanying statement
presenting a mixed picture. On one hand, the forecast included a
signiﬁcant upward revision to the inﬂation outlook; on the other, the
statement noted lower core inﬂation in October and an anticipated

economic slowdown in the third quarter of 2026. Notably, the NBP’s
data cut-oﬀ point was likely two weeks ago. Consequently, the
projections do not account for the downside surprise in October’s
inﬂation ﬁgures, while they do reﬂect record-high energy prices,
which, from today’s perspective, appear somewhat more
favourable. Governor Adam Glapiński’s press conference today is
expected to maintain a dovish tone, and we do not anticipate the
NBP’s ﬁrst rate hike until the ﬁrst quarter of 2027.

In the Czech Republic, we expect the CNB to hike rates by 25bp to
4.00%, the second such increase since the onset of the US-Iran
conﬂict. The CNB remains the most hawkish central bank in the CEE
region, and its new forecast is expected to reinforce this stance. The
updated projections will likely point to higher inﬂation, interest
rates, and wages, alongside a hawkish external environment, while
the GDP growth forecast will likely be revised downward. The CNB

has scaled back its forward guidance. We do not expect ﬁrm
commitments regarding future policy steps today, but rather an

indication that the door remains open for further hikes if necessary.
Given the rally in interest rates over the past few days, the threshold
for a "hawkish" surprise is lower. The market is currently pricing in

roughly 80bp of tightening, down from a peak of 125bp. That said,
the CNB has generally delivered less hawkishness this year than the
market anticipated, often resulting in a weaker currency and lower
interest rates following its meetings.
The global narrative following the US midterms remains robust, and
a sharp drop in oil prices could easily overshadow today’s
meetings of the NBP and the CNB. This sense of relief is supporting
CEE currencies and pushing rates lower. A dovish tone from the NBP

should further fuel the decline in rates, while the zloty will likely
retain room for recovery. The koruna is expected to underperform in

## Document 401: IT技术太烂，日本正在被黑客们当怪刷

Source PDF: all report pdf/IT技术太烂，日本正在被黑客们当怪刷.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⽇本企业近期遭遇⿊客攻击潮，短短数周数千万条会员信息⾯临泄露⻛险。 AI Agent 降低
⽹络攻击成本与技术⻔槛，叠加⽇本企业 IT 基础设施⽼旧、内部技术能⼒薄弱及外包依赖
严重，令漏洞利⽤与供应链攻击加速蔓延。
摘要：
“⿊⽇本公司，已经成⼀项运动了 ”
前⼏天， Reddit 上有个⽼哥发了这么⼀句感慨。起因是他刷到⼀⻚⽇本新闻，全都是 “XX 公司
被⿊客攻击，数据可能泄露 ” 。
⽐如  10 ⽉  5 号，连锁烤⾁店 “ 烧⾁  King” 道歉，说⾃家  App 有  1078 万多条会员信息可能泄
漏；
四天后，订机票酒店的  Skyticket ⽴⻢跟上，说俺也有  1464 万条信息可能泄露；
同⼀天，⼆⼿书巨头  Bookoff 也说可能有  643 万条数据外泄。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

短短  4 天，就有三家公司  3000 万条会员信息存在泄露⻛险。
⽽这，还只是冰⼭⼀⻆。
根据时事通信社和安全⼚商  CS2 汇总，从  9 ⽉  25 ⽇到  10 ⽉  9 ⽇，⽇本已有⼗⼏家知名企业
和机构宣布被⿊客⼊侵、数据疑似泄露。
好好好，现在鞠躬道歉都得组团了。。。
⾯对这场声势浩⼤的 “ ⿊客运动 ” ，⽇本官房⻓官只能表⽰还不清楚事件之间有⽆关联；⽽警察
厅的攻击⼿法调查，据说要到  2027 年  3 ⽉才出结果。
不是哥们⼉，真到那会纪录⽚都出第⼆季了吧。
看到这你可能好奇，怎么突然这么⼀⼤波⿊客⼊侵企业爆发呢？
要世超暴论，背后肯定是  AI。
虽然官⽅还没调查出来⿊客有没有⽤  AI ，但趋势已经很明显了：
⽇本警⽅之前就抓过好⼏个拿  ChatGPT ⼊侵⽇企的⻘少年； 10 ⽉  14 ⽇⽇本安全公司  Scutum
也发⽂实锤，⾄少有  3 个  IP 正在⽤  AI Agent 攻击⽇本企业⽹站，其中⼀个  IP 在  8 ⼩时内丝滑
切换了⼗⼏种⿊客⼯具。

### Page 3

可问题⼜来了， AI ⿊客满世界都是，怎么偏偏逮着⽇本企业往死⾥薅呢？
不好意思，这锅⼀半都得⽇本⼈⾃⼰背。
⾸先，很多⽇本企业的  IT 基础设施⽼旧，安全管理也⻓期落后。
9 ⽉  28 ⽇，漫画界扛把⼦集英社宣布被⿊，原因是旗下博主管理系统的  CMS 配置错误。 CMS
就是⽹站的内容后台，它留了个专⻔⼝⼦给程序对接⽤，本来要先核对⾝份再放⾏，结果设置
出错了，⿊客绕过检查给⾃⼰开了个管理员级别的账号。
就好⽐写字楼的⻔禁办卡机设置错了，本来只有物业能办卡，结果路⼈站过去刷⼀下，就给⾃
⼰办了张全楼通⾏卡。
⽇本警察厅统计，今年上半年就有  123 起勒索软件案，创下有统计以来的最⾼纪录，⽽⼀半受
害者的突破⼝都是  VPN 设备。
说实话，如果你⽤过⽇本⽹站，对这个结果不会意外。
那股扑⾯⽽来的千禧年⽓息，总能让⼈恍惚间以为穿越了回⼆⼗年前。
说直⽩点就是：企业的  IT 技术和意识，⾮常落后。。。
⽇本⽓象厅官⽹

### Page 4

有⽹友吐槽  2026 年了，⽇本很多⽹站的密码还不准⽤  * 、 ! 、 : 等特殊字符，⻓度限制在  8 到  12
位，⽣怕⿊客暴⼒破解时算⼒不够⽤；
还有⼈说有些⼤公司的密码和⽤⼾名⼀模⼀样，⽽且⾄今迷恋⼀种 “PPAP” 古⽼仪式，就是先给
你发⼀封加密  ZIP 压缩包邮件，紧接着再发第⼆封邮件，把解压密码明⽂发给你。
这  TM 和把钥匙压在⻔⼝脚垫下，再贴张纸条 “ 钥匙在脚垫下 ” 有什么区别啊啊？

### Page 5

也有⼈说，某家制造业公司死活不肯升级  ERP 系统（企业管理软件），让员⼯在虚拟机⾥接着
⽤  Windows 7 ，但  Win7 早在  2020 年就停⽌安全更新了啊。。。
看到这你可能会觉得困惑：⽇本不是科技强国吗？
⽇本强的是制造业，像汽⻋、相机、游戏机都能卖到全球；但软件系统，尤其企业⾃⼰内部⽤
的  IT 系统，⼏乎都交给了外包公司。
之前⽇本信息处理推进机构发过⼀组⽩⽪书。
在美国， 65.4% 的  IT ⼈才受雇于各⼤⽤⼾企业（⽐如银⾏、零售商、制造⼚⾃⼰养的技术团
队），只有  34.6% 在外包  IT 公司。
但⽇本完全是反过来的。
2015 年，⽇本有  72% 的  IT ⼈员在外包  IT 公司；到了  2020 年，这个⽐例是  73.6% ，还能
涨？！

### Page 6

这意味着：绝⼤多数⽇本传统⼤企业，内部⼏乎没有研发和运维能⼒。
这种信息不对称和繁琐的对接流程，造成企业在  IT 维护⽅⾯极其落后，⼏乎就是 “ 能⽤就别动
啦 ” 。
那这种⼤环境下，奇葩的密码规则和配置错误就不奇怪了。
更关键的是，这种全员外包的模式，在供应链攻击⾯前就是⼀个超级连环雷：只要⼀家底层外
包商被⿊客攻破，那它⾝上的⼤企业客⼾就会像多⽶诺⻣牌⼀样，⼀个接⼀个倒。
⽐如  10 ⽉  5 ⽇到  6 ⽇，⼤和证券、西铁城前后脚宣布数据可能泄露，就是因为给他们提供客服
系统的同⼀家外包商  被端了。
卡拉  OK 巨头  Big Echo 872 万条信息可能泄露，也是由于外包商⼀名员⼯的电脑中了病毒。
按理说⽇本企业  IT 这么⽼旧、安全管理这么落后，早该出事了，为啥偏偏是现在才集中爆雷
呢？

### Page 7

世超觉得是  AI 模型的能⼒⼜上升了⼀个台阶。
以前，⿊客们压根没那么多精⼒去⼀家家企业破解，哥们费⽼劲肯定是想吃点好的吧？很多企
业就这么靠 “ 性价⽐低 ” 撑到了今天。
但  AI ⼀来，谁跟你俩性价⽐？⿊客多打⼀家的边际成本⼏乎为零。
⽽且，过去那些扫描出漏洞之后的难题，什么利⽤漏洞，⿊⼊后找哪些数据呀、⼯具不管⽤该
换哪个，都得⿊客坐在电脑前⼀步步⼿动操作，⼀个⼈⼀天打不了⼏家。
但现在这些步骤也能交给  AI Agent 了， AI 可以  24 ⼩时⼀家接⼀家试下去。
针对⽇本电商  SQL 注⼊攻击，攻击源  IP 数量两⽉暴涨到  16 倍
其次，AI 也把当⿊客的⻔槛砍没了。
以前当⿊客要懂底层架构、懂汇编、懂漏洞挖掘；现在你只需要懂怎么跟  AI 聊天，就能拥有⼀
个⿊客的⾝份。
前⾯说过，⽇本警⽅已经抓了不少拿  AI 作案的赛博未成年。
2025 年  2 ⽉，东京警视厅逮捕了  3 名只有  14 到  16 岁的初⾼中⽣。他们⽤  AI 写了个⾮法登录
程序，登进别⼈的乐天移动账号，疯狂开通⼿机卡再转⼿倒卖。

12 ⽉，⽇本连锁⽹咖 “ 快活 CLUB” 宣布  729 万名会员信息泄露，后来才发现始作俑者是个  17 岁
的⼤阪男⾼中⽣，⽤  ChatGPT 改进了作案程序，作案群⾥甚⾄还有个⼩学六年级的⼩孩哥。
嗯， AI 实现了科技平权也实现了犯罪平权。
以前你是菜鸡，你只能在键盘上扣  666 ；现在你是菜鸡， AI ⼿把⼿教你写脚本进局⼦。
⼀边是  AI 技术的迅速发展，⼀边是稀烂的  IT 基础设施。
⽇本，就这么成了⿊客的新⼿村。

### Page 8

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

## Document 484: Westpac NZ Retail Spending Pulse, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Discretionary spending flat as high fuel
prices bite
• Per-person spending on Westpac issued credit and debit cards was up 1.2% in October and is 7.8%
higher than this time last year.
October spending.
• October’s rise in spending was mainly related to the sharp increase in petrol prices, with the average

price for 91-unleaded around the country climbing to a record high of over $3.60/ltr. That saw
spending on fuel rise 14% over the month.
• Those higher fuel prices, along with other cost of living pressures, have put the brakes on other
purchases. Spending in discretionary areas was down 0.4% in October and has essentially been
tracking sideways since May. There’s been notable softness in spending on travel and household
furnishings. However, spending in restaurants has been holding up.

Annual growth.
• While spending has certainly been constrained by the rise in living costs since the start of this year,
spending levels are still well above those we saw in 2025. And that’s not just because of high fuel
prices, with spending in discretionary areas also up compared to this time last year.
• That’s been supported by factors such as the drop in borrowing costs over the past year and
strength in export earnings. The boost from those factors is helping to balance the impacts of

factors such as softness in the labour market and high fuel prices. However, as we highlight over the
page, the strength in spending has been uneven across the country.

Regional breakdown
• As has been the case for some time, spending growth has been strongest in Southland. Other parts
of the country with strong rural backbones like Canterbury and Northland have also continued to
see solid gains.
• Spending growth remains more modest in Auckland. However, we expect that spending in Auckland

will take a step higher next month, with hospitality spending in particular likely to be boosted by
events like the Indian cricket tour, Diwali and Auckland Marathon.
• In a notable move, Wellington has shifted up the ranks and now sits in the middle of the pack.
Wellingtonians are no doubt holding their breath ahead of Saturday’s tight election and many will be
stocking up for the traditional election night barbecue and watch party.*

## Document 487: What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it_ (2026-10-11).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it?
A closer look at the impact of China’s currency on China’s export growth.
Sean Yong/Reuters
UPDATED
October 11, 2026 12:00 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
There is an argument—made in the recent Geneva
Report and by various former IMF officials—that
China’s current export surge is the simple
consequence of the housing downturn and the
resulting weakness in Chinese internal demand.*
The weakness in the RMB in this story is entirely
incidental to the increase in exports, which is, more or
less, a mechanical response to weak internal demand.

Cannot sell excavators at home? Export them.
Built more modern car capacity than your internal
market supports? Export that too…
The fall in the nominal RMB over the course of 2022
and the first half of 2023 in nominal and read terms
was simply a function of domestic macroeconomic
weakness, and a growing rate differential. It wasn’t an
active policy choice by China’s central bank.

### Page 3

3
Now, some might note that China’s currency doesn’t
always move with rate differentials. The RMB didn’t
consistently get pulled up when Chinese rates were
higher than U.S. rates (2002-2020 generally
speaking), and while it did fall when rate differentials
moved against the RMB in 2022 and 2023, it then
stabilized and has even appreciated modestly since
the middle of last year. That appreciation hasn’t been
big enough to correct the deep undervaluation of the

RMB, but it came at a time when the PBOC policy
rate (the reverse repo rate) was well below the U.S.
policy rate.
China doesn’t have an open financial account. As a
result, equilibrium in the foreign exchange market is
thus driven far more by exporter conversion and
current account flows than by rate differentials and
financial flows.** That, together with China’s ongoing
management of the exchange rate and a trade surplus
that can generate significant inflows, means that the

### Page 4

4
value of the RMB is much more of a policy variable
under the independent control of the Chinese
authorities than a simple byproduct of monetary and
fiscal policies.
The modern IMF is reluctant to accept this
conclusion. Its surveillance has generally ignored
China’s currency management (even if that
management is relatively obvious; see the data on FX
settlement) and its recent comprehensive assessment
of China’s economy completely ignored the balance of

payments and exchange rate management.
But it is pretty obvious to the market, even if the
PBOC, rather absurdly, insists that “market forces
play a decisive role in determining the RMB
exchange rate.”
I doubt the PBOC can win this argument. I agree
with Mark Sobel:
“The renminbi is still heavily managed and the
authorities have acted to slow appreciation. The paper
states that the PBoC phased out ‘regular’ foreign
exchange intervention in 2017. But it has many

means at its disposal to achieve the same effect
through extensive capital controls, administrative
guidance, signalling at the fix and deploying state-
owned commercial banks. These means are heavily
used. Thus, the word ‘regular’ must be taken with a
pinch of salt. The comparison to Japan, which

### Page 5

5
intervenes occasionally, is spurious – the yen largely
floats freely in integrated global markets. The same
cannot be said for the renminbi.”
In reality, the PBOC’s daily fix now plays the decisive
role in the currency’s path, and the market is often a
bet on how the PBOC’s management of the yuan will
evolve.
That is clear from the lack of volatility in the yuan’s
rate against the dollar. The yuan’s move down against
the dollar in 2022 was a controlled 10 percent move,

and the appreciation over the last 16 months has been
even more controlled crawl.
And the market is clearly massaged with substantial
help from the state banks. In fact that the pattern of
changes in the monthly FX settlement data looks
exactly like the pattern of intervention of a central
bank managing its currency around a tight band (the

### Page 6

6
state banks in practice buy FX to keep the CNY from
testing the strong side of the band, so there is an
asymmetry in how the CNY is managed)
None of this would matter much if China’s economy
was small. But that obviously isn’t the case. Nor would
this matter much if China’s exports didn’t respond to
changes in the RMB’s real (and nominal) value. But
that is also not the case.
There is abundant evidence that China’s exports do
respond to moves in the currency. Periods of RMB

weakness have consistently been associated with
periods of especially rapid Chinese export growth.

### Page 7

7
Chancellor Merz and President Macron got this
right, even if macroeconomic issues haven’t yet
figured prominently in the EU’s dialogue with
China.*** As they note in their open letter to the
European Commission:
“An appreciation of undervalued currencies towards a value
more in line with fundamentals would help support the
rebalancing of trade accounts with the EU in the short and
medium term … European policy discussions focused on
forging a common understanding of these exchange rate issues

and their interaction with global imbalances would be useful.”
The best evidence that exports responded to changes
in the currency, and not simply to changes in the path
of China’s internal demand, is the acceleration in
China’s export growth after the RMB’s depreciation
over the course of 2022.
This is the killer chart.

### Page 8

8
Now, you might ask: what of the acceleration in
export growth in 2020 and 2021? That was pretty
clearly tied to the global increase in goods demand
that came with the pandemic and work from home
requirements. It was expected to fade when the world
economy normalized. But it didn’t. By 2023 Chinese
export growth again topped global trade growth.
The general expectation that the rise in China’s goods
surplus during Covid would prove to be “transitory”

### Page 9

9
heavily influenced the IMF’s work at the time: see the
IMF’s external sector reports of 2023 and 2024.
I would argue that neglecting the exchange rate was
the key reason the IMF’s kept forecasting, incorrectly,
that China’s current account surplus would fall (See
this blog with Shahin Vallee). The IMF certainly
didn’t warn that China’s property downturn and the
RMB’s real depreciation risked a second China shock
in its 2022, 2023, or 2024 surveillance reports.

Concretely, the weaker RMB appears to have
contributed to a very large outperformance of China’s
exports relative to global trade. See the following
chart
That incidentally also happened after the RMB
followed the dollar down in 2003.
In both periods, there were significant structural
shifts. The WTO accession in 2002, and the success

### Page 10

10
of Chinese industrial policies in the “green” industrial
sectors and autos in 2022. In both cses exports were
turbocharged when structural shifts that should have
resulted in a stronger currency were instead amplified
by currency weakness.
No matter. Paul Krugman has noted that a country
that is converging technologically with its trade
partners should see a trend appreciation in its
currency. China got the technological convergence,
but not the real appreciation—at least not until this

year. And the move since the middle of 2025 has
corrected the depreciation of the RMB that occurred
when the RMB followed the dollar down on
liberation day, but not the structural overvaluation.
Now, there is an argument that exchange rate
adjustment is too hot an issue to make it a priority.
The PBOC isn’t interested it seems.
And some believe that in the interest of comity and
good relations with China, the IMF should mirror
the PBOC’s views. After all a stronger RMB would

slow China’s exports and thus slow China’s economy
at a time when China needs export growth to escape
deflation and generate employment. Or so the
argument goes. Concerns about “external balance“
and the negative global spillovers from China’s rising
goods surplus should be ignored and the Fund’s sole
focus should be on policies that lead to internal

### Page 11

11
balance. That provides one rationale for the Fund’s
ongoing decision to essentially ignore the contentious
issue of the PBOC’s ongoing exchange rate
management.
That argument, however, assumes that China’s
economy is small and thus China’s external surplus
can safely be ignored because it isn’t a drag on the
growth of the IMF’s other members.
That clearly isn’t true.
And it isn’t clear that China is more interested in
having a negotiation over its level of basic pension

benefits or the size of its fiscal deficit (where the
IMF continues to make timid recommendations that
would not move the needle—and certainly wouldn’t
meaningfully reduce China’s external surplus) than a
discussion over its currency practices.****
Back in the world of fixed but adjustable exchange
rates, there was an expectation (not always met to be
sure) that surplus countries would allow their
currencies to strengthen to bring their economies

back into external balance, and that other policy tools
would be used to ensure internal balance.
I would argue that China’s exchange rate (against
both the dollar and the basket) has many similarities
to the old world of fixed but adjustable currencies.
True, it moves on a controlled path when it moves

### Page 12

12
between different levels rather than jumping up and
down. But the PBOC does frequently target a level,
and that targeted level does change over time.
I would further argue that steps like the recent tariff
rate quota that will limit China’s exports of hybrid
cars to Europe to around 350,000 units next year (up
from 2025, but well below the 800,000 units of
2026) aren’t going to address the underlying
imbalance on their own. They can provide a bit of a

buffer for specific sectors.
But that buffer, while necessary, won’t address the
broad-based erosion of manufacturing capacity
outside China. This erosion will persist as long as an
undervalued RMB sends the entire world a signal to
use China as its production base.
The new system of managed trade that is emerging in
autos would need to be progressively expanded to an
ever-increasing set of sectors if protection is used as a
substitute for both domestic reform in China and

exchange rate adjustment.
That is now the direction of travel, but it doesn’t
address the underlying systemic issues.
Incidentally, those who believe the path of internal
demand is critical to rebalancing and that exchange
rate moves are secondary need to explain how an
acceleration in domestic demand would, on its own,

### Page 13

13
lead China’s external accounts to adjust. One
important, stylized fact about China is that domestic
demand growth is no longer feeding over to import
growth (ex-gold, that is).
Bottom line: China, which needs a 1.5 percentage
point contribution from net exports every year, is on a
collision course with its trading partners without a
currency correction that provides the basis for more
balanced trade even in the absence of trade
restrictions.
And there isn’t a realistic path towards balance that

doesn’t involve a stronger RMB; there aren’t, as Paul
Krugman once put it, immaculate transfers.
* That downturn shows up in domestic deflationary
pressure, and weak job growth/high rates of (at least
youth) unemployment. China, in other words, isn’t in
“internal balance.” I really liked the report from

### Page 14

14
Kamakshya Trivedi and Hui Shan of Goldman Sachs
arguing China needs two tools to hit two targets
(internal and external balance).
** To be sure, rate differentials matter for exporter
conversion—but so does the expected movement of
the RMB. The market understands this better than
the Fund. The Chen and Suwanapruti (Goldman
Sachs) note uses a framework similar to mine to
describe the CNY’s moves in Q3:
“In our view, sizeable FX inflows have continued to support

RMB appreciation despite unfavorable interest rate
differentials, as strong export receipts and increased exporter
FX conversions have outweighed portfolio outflows. Our
estimated FX conversion ratio rose to 63% in August,
compared with around 45% in normal years, likely reflecting a
shift in market expectations from RMB depreciation to
appreciation.”
*** Š ef č ovi č  has jurisdiction over trade policy
(narrowly defined), but not broader macroeconomic

policies. Europe’s institutional architecture for
exchange rate coordination is not well-developed.
**** The current account surplus is no longer masked
to the same extent as in 2023 and 2024, as SAFE has
fiddled with its BoP methodology once again (per the
Federal Reserve) and the BoP goods surplus is no
longer significantly below the customs goods surplus.
Of course, there are still problems in the data—the
deficit in the investment income account most

電子製造業 ESG 評等分配圖

瑞昱 2379 TT  ( 電子上游–IC 設計 ) 
網通規格逐步推進，待評價面更具吸引力 
投資與評價 
1. PC 表現雖較為疲弱，但受惠基建升級與高頻寬解決方案的挹注下，網通
產品需求優於預期，預估 26 年營收年增 21.8% 
2. 規格升級仍將為主要驅動力，AI 產品亦添柴火，但推估傳統應用在高基
期下成長性有限，預估 27 年營收年增 9.7%。 
3. 預估瑞昱 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 31.91/38.15 元，考量成長動能及金融
環境不確定性，以 27 年獲利預估乘上本益比區間上緣 20 倍為評價基
礎，目標價 760 元(20X FY27E EPS)，股價尚未具顯著吸引力，維持中
立投資評等。 
重點更新 
網通產品維持韌性，表現略優於預期，預估 2026 營收年增 21.8% 
網通需求受惠基礎建設持續升級與高頻寬解決方案的挹注，其中乙太網路隨
路由器、中小企業網路及伺服器採用擴大而成長，網管型交換器在企業、中
小企業及伺服器控制層應用持續提升市占 ， 與非網管型產品的差距逐漸縮小 。
Wi-Fi 方面，Wi-Fi 7 在 PC 與路由器的滲透率，預估今年約達 30%，在車用
乙太網路採用持續穩定擴大，今年營收亦呈穩定成長態勢。但在消費性電子
方面，因 DRAM 與 CPU 等零組件持續漲價推升零售價格，削弱消費性與入
門級購買力，提前拉貨效應亦趨緩，使得PC 表現研究部預估年減約一成，另
外電視出貨在年中世足賽備貨後放緩，零組件成本壓力也持續存在 。綜合考
量下，整體預估瑞昱 2026 營收年增 21.8%達 1494.6 億元。 
2027 年規格升級及 AI 為主要驅動力，但評價仍不具吸引力 
Wi-Fi 方面，Wi-Fi 7 的滲透率，預估 2027 年將達 40%，新一代 Wi-Fi 8 開
發進度順利，預計 2H27 量產，乙太網路與 10G PON 採用擴大，帶動產品
組合改善，高階網管型交換器預估也將取得更多 design win。目前伺服器產
品涵蓋管理連接與周邊功能，包括乙太網路與交換器、 SSD 控制器、 I/O 
bridge 及 USB hub 控制器，並以此為基礎進入更高速的伺服器互連，推估
2027 年營收佔比將達一成水準 。100G 光通訊解決方案正進行認證，預期
2027 年放量更明顯，400G 與 800G 解決方案開發則穩定進行，預估 2H27
推出，PC 則預估呈現持平表現。整體而言，預估 27 年營收年增 9.7%，達
1639.1 億元，以評價面來考量，目前股價仍尚未具顯著吸引力。

資料來源：台灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 2

訪談內容 
網通產品規格升級+AI 將為長期成長動能 
2026 年 Wi-Fi 7 在 PC/NB 市場滲透率達 30%，約為 2025 年的兩倍，高階商用機型導入最積
極，Wi-Fi 聯盟目標 2027 年 6 月前完成認證測試計畫，並於 2027 年 9 月正式啟動 Wi-Fi 8 認
證。正積極與客戶進行Wi-Fi 8 design-in，預期產品 2H27 開始放量，客戶產品將在正式認證後
於 2028 年更廣泛上市。Wi-Fi 8 在 Wi-Fi 7 的基礎上著重實際使用環境中的極高可靠度，以及更
低、更穩定的延遲，預估將成為廣泛採用且生命周期長的平台。

車用乙太網路在新車生產的滲透率已超過 50%，全球汽車出貨持平，成長並非依賴車輛出貨增
加，而是來自單車乙太網路埠數結構性增加、持續打入新車廠客戶，以及穩定的 design win 動
能。隨車廠電子電機架構演進，需求自然轉向更高密度的安全關鍵解決方案，產品路線圖與此趨
勢一致，正擴充更高整合度、更多埠數的車用交換器，並開發先進閘道控制器，以因應未來軟體
定義汽車的需求。

另外 AI ASIC 機會目前處於與客戶接觸的初期階段 ， 基礎為高速連接與介面IP 的優勢 。 邊緣AI(含
地端伺服器與邊緣伺服器)是推動規格升級、提升單機價值的結構性機會，而非僅依賴PC 需求復
甦的成長動能。邊緣 AI 對更高頻寬、更低延遲、更佳功耗效率與更豐富多模態互動的需求，可
為產品組合創造更高價值的解決方案。

供應端部分預期 2027 年改善將較明顯，工程端也導入 design for availability 原則，在可行範
圍內降低對受限物料的依賴。成本漲幅已超過可合理吸收的程度，與客戶逐案討論價格調整。

### Page 3

瑞昱 ESG 政策 
瑞昱主要從事 IC 設計，產業類別歸類在半導體行業中，依據專門為投資人設計的永續揭露框架 SASB
準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水管理、廢棄物和危險材料
管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材料採購與效率。由於瑞昱
為 IC 設計公司，研究部將篇幅著重在產品設計面，並因員工對於IC 設計公司而言是相當重要的資產，
故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述，其餘溫室氣體排放及產品設計與生命週期管理等則於後簡述。

1. 員工健康與安全： 
瑞昱重視及關心同仁的身體健康與生活狀態，以優於法令之規定，每年定期實施免費健檢服務。對於健
康檢查結果為重大 ( 含輕度 ) 異常同仁，依異常項目、病情及個人健康情形，於複檢前、後分別安排
廠醫諮詢及衛教說明，持續追蹤健檢結果。與新竹臺大分院新竹醫院合作之健康諮詢服務，每月安排 9 
場次駐廠醫師健康諮詢服務，每場次由專業醫師提供  3 小時諮詢服務，2022 年度共計服務 384 人
次。

圖 1 歷年健檢人數統計

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 員工多元化和包容性： 
2022 年瑞昱全集團全球員工人數為 6,828 人，海外員工註人數佔總 員工人數約 36.9%，其中 72% 
以上同仁擁有碩士、博士學歷。瑞昱半導體以臺灣為主要營運據點， 臺灣地區員工人數為 4,943 人，
其中正式員工人數 4,826 人，約聘員工人數 117 人，男性員工所佔 比例約 80.46%、女性員工所佔
比例約 19.54%，其中 77% 以上同仁擁有碩士、博士學歷。在 2022 年所聘僱的人力中，無論在正
式員工、男性員工、女性員工等類別指標，皆有明顯成長。 
圖 2 近三年員工人數

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 4

3. 溫室氣體排放： 
瑞昱在 2022 年溫室氣體排放共計 26,462.8729 tCO2e ，其中直接排放(範疇 1)為 808.4856 tCO2e，
分別為固定排放源(緊急發電機之柴油)、移動排放源(公務車之汽油)及逸散排放源(冷媒、化糞池、滅火
器)，瑞昱因無生產製程，故無製程排放源；間接排放(範疇 2)為 25654.3873 tCO2e，主要來源為外購
電力，占整體排放量 96.9%。

圖 3 溫室氣體排放量、強度

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

4. 水和廢水管理： 
瑞昱於 2022 年持續成功開發出多項新一代產品，在各種應用功耗 （瓦數） 表現上比前一代產品功耗減
少、面積也縮小，在長時間及大量傳輸時更具顯著降低耗能之效果，充份體現節能與對環境之友善，並
帶給終端用戶更佳的使用體驗。

科技財報仍以AI展望最樂觀

科技產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/30
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《AI晶片與相關硬體獲利持續創新高》
根據機構LSEG統計，受到AI晶片、記憶體、硬碟等
科技公司獲利優於預期，機構上調科技產業4Q26、
1Q27獲利年增率到56.2%、54.6%，本行樂觀看待
科技產業發展後勢 。10月份重要科技硬體晶圓代工
龍頭台積電3Q26法說表示，受益於強勁AI需求與2
奈米開始貢獻營收，公司3Q26營收與獲利均優於市
場預期。3Q26高效運算與智慧手機營收比重分別達
68%、21%。高效運算營收年增高達 53%、智慧手
機年減10%。AI代理與開源模型普及帶動先進製程

用量 ， 台 積 電 預 估4Q26 美 元 營 收 將 年 增62% ，
2026年美元營收預估達44%。由於AI晶片成長高於
60%，預估台積電2027年營收與獲利仍將續創新高。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
科技 正向 台積電、英特爾、類比晶片財報顯示資料中心需求仍遠優於消費電子 終端，企業對於AI相關軟體支出持續增溫
• 雲端巨頭資本支出持平，伺服器
仍優於消費終端 Pg2
• 類比晶片終端需求上升 ，工業、
資料中心展望佳 Pg3
• 券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期
持續增溫 Pg4
• IT 支出首選AI/ML/自動化相關 Pg5

2
重點摘要
• 雲端巨頭資本支出持平 ，伺
服器仍優於消費終端
《雲端巨頭資本支出持平 ，伺服器仍優於消費終端 》
3Q26財報顯示 ，谷哥 、亞馬遜 、微軟與 Meta在
2026年資本支出目標合計達 7,300億美元 ，較上季
略減25億美元(-0.3%)，減少主要是微軟外部第三方
算力產能延遲導致資本支出目標下修 50億美元，與
Meta上調25億美元相互抵消 。雖然雲端巨頭支出
持平但資料中心晶片需求仍強勁。英特爾3Q26財報
顯示，受益於資料中心與 AI事業帶動營收達 165億
美元、年增21%。其中資料中心與人工智慧相關營
收73億美元續創歷史新高、年增78%，2027年相關
晶片仍將供不應求 。反觀個人電腦營收 81億美元、

年減5%。英特爾預估 2026年個人電腦出貨量將年
減12%、2027年受到記憶體與儲存供給影響預估將
續減10%。
受到上半年消費者預期終端售價上漲提前換機，
3Q26個人電腦與智慧手機出貨均呈現大幅下滑。根
據IDC統計，3Q26全球個人電腦出貨量達6,270萬
台，較2025年同期衰退20%。同樣受到記憶體漲價
與供應短缺衝擊的智慧手機3Q26出貨量2.6億台，
較2025年同期衰退22%。由於未來記憶體與儲存價
格小幅調漲以及供給受限下，消費終端價格仍將維
持高價位。因此，IDC預估個人電腦與智慧手機
2027年將高機率維持衰退，預估要2028年出貨量
才有望復甦。
資料來源：公司，2026/10/30，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/30

吸納量
重點摘要
3
《類比晶片終端需求上升 ，工業、資料中心占比較
高供應商營收更具成長性》
德州儀器(TXN)3Q26營收60.6億美元、EPS 2.62美
元雙雙優於預期，4Q26財測營收58-63億美元，中
位數較市場普遍預期高出 6%，反映資料中心市場
需求持續成長 ，使營收預測優於往年季節性回落 。
以3Q26終端市場來看 ，工業營收年增超過 40%、
季增約10%，成長來自航太國防 、能源基礎設施等
領域；車用營收年增約 15%、季增約10%，受益於
客戶庫存水準降低及電動車占比提高；資料中心營
收則年增約95%、季增20%，為成長最快的終端市
場。在營運方面，產能利用率提高以及2027年潛在
的價格調漲可能進一步擴大獲利成長空間 ，儘管資

本支出可能高於2026年的20~30億美元，但顯示終
端需求正擴大成長。
恩智浦(NXPI)3Q26營收37.9億美元、EPS 4.20美元
優於預期 ，但4Q26財測營收中位數 38.5億美元略
低於市場預期 。以3Q26終端市場來看 ，車用營收
20.7億美元、年增13%，成長主要來自軟體定義汽
車(SDV)產品貢獻；工業與物聯網營收 8.02億美元 、
年增39%，主要由資料中心 、工廠自動化和醫療保
健等產業帶動；通訊基礎設施營收 5.03億美元、年
增54%，得益於資料中心業務成長 。恩智浦財報結
果顯示各終端市場需求持續上升 ，惟成長速度較慢
的車用營收占比超過五成 ，未來成長速度可能不及
工業和資料中心營收占比更高的同業。
• 德州儀器 4Q26 財 測 營 收 較
市場預期高出6%，反映資料
中心市場需求大幅成長。
• 德儀產能利用率提高及 2027
年潛在的價格調漲可能進一
步擴大獲利成長空間。
• 恩智浦3Q26營收、EPS優於
市場預期 ，各終端市場營收
皆呈年增。

• 恩智浦 4Q26 財測低 於預期 ，
反映其成長速度較慢的車用
營收占比超過五成。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/28，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/30

零售客流量表
• 券商 CIO 調查顯示 IT 採購預
算與軟體支出預期持續增溫。
• AI/ML 與網安軟體優先性持續
領先，但 ERP、BI、CRM 等
應用軟體優先性也見改善。
4
《券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期持續增溫》
券商 CIO 調查指出 2027 年的企業 IT 採購預算仍維
持成長。CIO 現在預計 2026 年的 IT 採購預算將年
增 4.0%，自 2Q26 走高 20bps，連續 3 次增溫。
對於 2027 年 IT 採購預算的首次估計，CIO 預期年
增 4.0%，雖僅與更新後的 2026 年預期相符，但實
則是近 4 年來的開年新高。在這之中，軟體預算成
長優於領先硬體、網通與服務，預期年增 4.4%，且

自 2026 年加速 10bps。2027 年軟體支出的預期加
速可能進一步說明短期內 AI 並未帶來全面性衝擊。
資料來源：摩根史丹利，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/20
人工智慧與機器學習 (AI/ML) 的支出優先性仍穩居
CIO 調查的第一名，佔比雖較前幾期調研回落，但
這應該是因為 CIO 對於開始增加其他項目的優先性。
AI 相關專案的預算仍最不可能被刪除，且淨占比
(即最不可能被刪除 – 最可能被刪除的比例)，3Q26
較 2Q26 大幅走揚 13ppts 至 17%。網路安全的支
出優先性則連 4 季走揚，且最不可能被刪除的淨占
比也走揚 1ppt 至 16%。持續反映出 AI 帶動的攻擊
面擴大、攻擊手法更加多元，與攻擊成本大幅走低，
導致企業在 AI 顯著產生正面效益前，優先選擇防範
未然的心態。而隨著 AI 的能力仍在快速發展，如近
期 Anthropic 研究即指出 AI 已可自我迭代，且小
型模型未來可能具備在物聯網裝置上自我複製的能

力，本行預期網安的需求可望仍持續強健。數位轉
型雖仍處第 3，但在本次券商調查中大幅地低於過
硬體 軟體 通訊設備 IT服務
■3Q25 ■4Q25 ■1Q26 ■2Q26 ■3Q26
2026 外部 IT 支出期望

去數季，且相較過往也是較低水位。相反地，本次

評等由正向下調為中立。

《貨幣政策-聯準會偏鷹發言但不急於連續升息》
10月FOMC會議如市場預期維持政策利率在3.75%-
4.0%不變，本次會議的重點可歸納如下：
◼ 聯準會認為美國經濟非常強勁 ，消費支出健康 ，
企業投資強勁 ，通膨仍處於偏高水準 ，「但也
不急於立刻升息 」。華許的解釋並不是通膨風
險已經消失 ，而是認為先前的政策行動已經透
過金融條件產生緊縮效果 ，投資人開始更加審
慎地配置資金，部分資產的信用利差擴大。
◼ 縮減資產負債表不是當前政策的優先選項 ，相
關問題仍交由工作小組研究 ，這表示雖然他在
長期制度設計上支持重新檢討聯準會資產負債
表的規模與功能 ，也不代表短期內就會立即推
動更激進的量化緊縮。
雖然華許一如既往地不提供對未來利率路徑的承諾 ，

但從記者會的發言可大致推論 ，目前的方案是間隔
一次會議再採取下一個新的行動 ，本次會議暫停升
息，12月會議將進行下一次升息 。2027年1月的會
議按兵不動，3月會議視市場情況再決定。本行的基
準情境是，2027年開始會看到關稅效應 、能源衝擊
對通膨的影響逐漸消退 ，因此不認為 2027年3月有
升息的必要性 。風險則在於美伊衝突進一步升級 ，
使企業利用年初例行漲價的機會將成本壓力轉嫁給
消費者，若此，3月可能再升息一次。但本行仍定調
9月以來的升息決策為「風險管理式的升息」，而非
持續性的升息循環 。加上華許並未將縮減資產負債
表列入優先事項，過度緊縮的風險不大。
重點摘要
• 本行仍定調9月以來的升息決
策為 「風險管理式的升息 」，
而非持續性的升息循環 。加
上華許並未將縮減資產負債

《總經情勢和殖利率-景氣穩健但不到過熱程度，美
債壓力有緩和跡象》
美國3Q26 GDP季增年率3.4%，高於2Q26的2.2%，
主要受惠於消費表現優於預期 。在油價高漲 、利率
上揚的環境當中 ，美國消費的強勁程度相當出乎意
料。其實個人所得成長率 、就業動能，都僅是溫和
成長，整體消費的動能 ，和股市帶來的財富效應息
息相關。數據顯示 ，美國家庭持有的股權價值已接
近年稅後所得的 3倍。但這也帶來一些疑慮 ，如k型
消費、景氣對股市的敏感度太高等 。綜合來看 ，美
國的經濟表現不錯，但並不到過熱程度。
美國10年期公債殖利率自 7月開始一波上行趨勢 ，
10月上旬一度升破 5.3%，但近期已回落到 5%之下，
一部分原因是市場對貨幣政策的共識已經開始凝聚 ，

以一季進行一次利率調整為原則 ，目前的經濟數據
尚不足以改變這點；另外原因是如同華許所述 ，長
率上升、金融條件往收緊的方向 ，已經透過融資成
本、信用利差 、投資人風險偏好來影響資本配置 。
當然這並不意味著公債利率將開啟下行趨勢 ，地緣
風險和財政問題仍不時帶來干擾 ，不過至少就近期
走勢而言 ，美債市場面臨的壓力已有初步緩和跡象 。
重點摘要
• 美國的經濟表現不錯 ，但並
不到過熱程度 。市場對貨幣
政策的共識已經開始凝聚 ，
以一季進行一次利率調整為
原則 ，美債市場面臨的壓力
有初步緩和跡象
2
資料來源: BCA，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10
消費動能越來越受財富效應影響
各輪景氣循環中，資產價值增幅占可支配所得的比例

《美股盤勢-景氣穩健但不過熱，美股短線有壓然無
礙趨勢》
科技類股於10月中旬出現明顯修正 ，尤其半導體相
關類股面臨較大的估值壓力 ，不過市場資金並未明

本行先前提及 「美股通常會在升息初期承壓 ，但若
非如同2022年般的連續性升息，股市的趨勢不至於
受到影響 」，此定調仍延續 。雖然市場不時會處於
「壞消息才是好消息 」的情緒當中 ，看到表現不錯
的經濟數據反而造成市場的震盪修正 ，但長期來說 ，
穩健的經濟基本面才是股市行情延續的關鍵 ，而目
前的景氣和企業獲利支持這樣的趨勢 ，只是高利率
環境仍將使市場資金更加嚴格看待資產的成長性 ，
具有強健資產負債表使其較不易受到融資成本上升

影響、具防禦性或差異化成長動能的企業 ，將能維
持相對韌性。
重點摘要
• 雖然市場不時會處於 「壞消

《拉美股市-巴西魯拉意外逆轉勝選，市場重新定價
財政前景，股市評等下調為中立》
巴西總統大選出現戲劇性逆轉 ，現任左翼總統魯拉
在10月初第一輪投票落後的情況下 ，於第二輪決選
中逆轉，擊敗右翼對手小波索納洛 ，這是1989年恢
復總統直選以來首次出現第二輪決選逆轉 ，巴西股
債匯市均出現劇烈下跌。
並不能說魯拉當選對巴西的經濟不利 ，他的前次當
選也並未引起股市震盪 。巴西股市在估值的吸引力 、
礦產的發展 ，都仍具有長線優勢 。然目前資本市場
確實較青睞較為親美和親商的波索納洛，巴西股市8
月中以來的漲幅不小 ，很大程度是反映波索納洛取
得優勢，因此市場需要修正先前的定價 ，外資撤出。
另外，魯拉第一輪投票後為挽救選情 ，承諾大量財

政政策，如擴大所得稅扣除額 、提高家庭補助金等 ，
在全球主要國家多面臨財政風險之際 ，市場對財政
前景的檢視力道將加大 ，利率上升壓力將提高 。據
此，將拉美股市和巴西股市的評等由正向下調為中
立。
重點摘要
• 巴西魯拉意外逆轉勝選 ，市
場重新定價財政前景 ，利率
上升壓力將提高 。據此 ，將
拉美股市和巴西股市的評等
由正向下調為中立。

## Document 566: フィリピン中銀、原油高とペソ安の「二重苦」に直面し、大幅利上げに舵 ～インフレ圧力の高まりを受けて利上げ幅を拡大、金融引_efcf5aff

Source PDF: all report pdf/フィリピン中銀、原油高とペソ安の「二重苦」に直面し、大幅利上げに舵 ～インフレ圧力の高まりを受けて利上げ幅を拡大、金融引_efcf5aff.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/2 
フィリピン中銀、原油高とペソ安の「二重苦」に直面し、大幅利上げに舵 
～インフレ圧力の高まりを受けて利上げ幅を拡大、金融引き締めの長期化も～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ フィリピン中銀は、10 月 22 日の定例会合で政策金利を50bp 引き上げ、5.50％とすることを
決定した。４会合連続の利上げとなり、今回は利上げ幅を拡大するなど、金融引き締めの度
合いを強化した。背景には、イラン情勢の悪化に伴う原油高や、金融市場における通貨ペソ
安の進行を受けてインフレ圧力が強まったことがある。９月のインフレ率は前年比＋7.2％と
中銀の目標を大幅に上回っている。中銀はインフレ率が 2027 年も高水準で推移し、2028 年
に目標中央値の３％に近付くとの見通しを示した。レモロナ総裁は、今回の利上げが為替安
定を目的とするものではないと強調する一方、追加利上げや緊急利上げの可能性を排除しな
い姿勢を示した。先行きもイラン情勢の不透明感やペソ安圧力が続く可能性があり、さらな
る金融引き締めを迫られる展開も予想される。

フィリピン中央銀行は、10 月 22 日に開催した定例の金融政策委員会において、政策金利である
翌日物リバースレポ金利を 50bp 引き上げ、5.50％とすることを決定した。同行は、４月の定例会
合で約２年半ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、その後も６月、 ８月の定例会合で追加利上げを実施してお
り、今回で４会合連続の利上げとなる。さらに、今回は利上げ幅を拡大させるなど金融引き締めの
度合いを強めており、政策金利も2025 年６月以来の高い水準となった。 
背景には、イラン情勢の悪化による原
油高が世界的なエネルギーインフレを招
くなか、同国は原油需要の９割以上を輸
入に依存しており、その影響に晒されや
すいことがある。直近９月のインフレ率
は前年比＋7.2％と中銀の目標レンジ （２
～４％）を大きく上回っている（図１） 。
さらに、原油などエネルギー資源価格の
高止まりは輸入額の増加を通じて対外収
支を悪化させており、金融市場では通貨
ペソの対ドル相場が最安値を更新するな

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
ど、輸入物価の押し上げがインフレ圧力を一段と増幅させる懸念も高まっている。このため、中銀
としては物価安定に向けた『より強力な』対応を示す必要があるとの判断から、今回は市場予想を
上回る大幅利上げに舵を切ったと考えられる。 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 先行きの物価動向について 「今後数ヵ月は一段の加速が見込まれ
る」としたうえで、 「インフレ率は2027 年も高水準で推移し、2028 年には目標の中央値 （３％） に
近付く」 との見通しを示した。 さらに、 「コアインフレ率も上昇が予想され、 潜在的なインフレ圧力
の高まりを示唆している」 との見方を示した。 その理由として、 「イラン情勢の再燃による原油価格
の上昇」 、 「交通運賃の上昇によるインフレの上振れリスクが高まっている」ほか、 「エルニーニョ
現象による農業生産や価格へのリスク」を挙げた。一方で、景気動向について「先行きは財政支援
による回復が見込まれる」との見通しを示した。 
今回の決定については 「インフレ圧力の高まりと、 さらなる上振れリスクに対して強力な政策対
応の必要性を認識した」との考えを示した。先行きについて「今回の対応が金融環境を適切に抑制
的なものにする」 、 「副次的な影響を含めてインフレ期待をしっかりと抑える」 との見通しを示した。
また、先行きの政策運営については「物価安定という主要任務に基づいて、インフレ率を３％に戻
すべくあらゆる措置を講じる用意がある」 と過去数会合と同じ文言を載せており、 追加利上げに含
みを持たせた。 
会合後の記者会見に臨んだレモロナ総裁は、 物価見通しについて 「これまでの想定に比べて悪い」
との見方を示した。今回の決定を受けた政策スタンスについて「抑制的であるが、十分に抑制的で
あるかは定かでない」 と追加利上げに含
みを持たせた。 今回の利上げについて
「ペソ相場の安定を目的としたもので
はない」 、 「われわれは多少為替介入を実
施しているが、 相場の乱高下を防ぐこと
を目的としたもの」として、為替安定の
ための利上げではないと付言した。 とは
いえ、 「ペソ安はインフレを招く恐れが
あり、懸念している」と述べるなど、そ
の動向を注視する姿勢をみせた （図２） 。
先行きの政策運営について 「さらなる利
上げの可能性を排除しない」、「利上げ幅については 25bp か 50bp、それ以上かは言えない」としつ
つ、 「緊急利上げの必要性も議論された」 とした。こうした姿勢は、 中銀が切迫した状況に直面して
いることを示唆している。イラン情勢は見通しが立ちにくく、ペソ相場についても下落圧力に晒さ
れる展開が続く可能性があり、その動向には注意が必要である。 
以 上

## Document 606: 中金：美股跌到位了么？

Source PDF: all report pdf/中金：美股跌到位了么？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

中⾦认为，虽然纳斯达克、费半估值已回落⾄历史 44% 和 14% 分位数，但市场缺乏关键催
化： AI 收⼊落后于投资，五⼤云⼚商⾃由现⾦流转负⾄ -318 亿美元，边际 ROIC 降⾄
6.1% ；传统需求⼜受⾼利率压制。若 AI 额外盈利增⻓被抹去，标普 500 仍有约 10% 下⾏空
间。明年⼀季度是关键节点 —— 届时美联储转向、四季报兑现、新国会就任将同步验证。
摘要：
美股近期波动明显放⼤，中期选举后的政策 “ 噪⾳ ” 诱发了市场动荡（《⺠主党 “ 横扫 ” 的资产含
义》），关键也是 AI ⾃⾝当前⾯临需求 “ ⻘⻩不接 ” 、投资⾼企等诸多 “ 问题 ” ，被提前暴露《如何看
待美股⼤跌？》）。
当然，这种快速的出清对⻓期⾛势⽽⾔或不是坏事，有助于消化过⾼的预期和仓位，这对未来
在新催化出现下重拾上⾏反⽽有利，只不过需要等待新的催化出现。要知道，今年以来的美股
涨幅基本都是盈利贡献的，⽽⾮依靠估值扩张，经过近期回调后，估值更是快速⾛低。除道琼
斯 19.6 倍估值仍在过去 20 年历史分位数的 86% ，标普 500 、纳斯达克估值分别回落⾄ 17.8 倍和

20.3 倍，对应 70% 和 44% 过去 20 年分位数，费半甚⾄降⾄ 13.8 倍，仅为 14% 的过去 20 年分位
数。
图表 1 ：标普 500 、纳斯达克等估值均已明显回落，费半甚⾄降⾄ 14% 历史分位数
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
今年美国基本⾯的 “K 型分化 ” 使得市场集中并依赖少数⽅向： 1 ）美国 Mag7 贡献了 2026 年以来
标普 500 涨幅的 22% ， 10 ⽉中旬下跌前⼀度贡献 40% ；2 ）五⼤云⼚商 2026 年前三季度盈利占标
普 500 指数的 20% ，贡献了标普 500 盈利增⻓的 25% ；3 ）⻰头集中效应也依然存在，年初以来标
普 500 上涨 8% ，但在 200 ⽇均线的股票⽐例却由年初的 60% 下降⾄ 55% ， 8-9 ⽉背离尤为明显。
然⽽，当前 AI 缺乏模型和应⽤层⾯催化、还遇上了政策不确定性，传统需求则受⾼利率压制。换
⾔之，主⼒⽅向⾯临挑战，传统⽅向尚待接棒，解释了整体的动荡。
图表 2 ：美国 Mag7 贡献了 2026 以来标普 500 涨跌幅的 22%

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部

### Page 3

所以，市场需要两个催化剂：要么 AI 出现巨⼤突破，要么美联储结束紧缩甚⾄转向宽松。在此
之前，我们建议谨慎观望：1 ）关注短期是否有流动性负反馈，或进⼀步政策扰动（新国会明年
初到任前的博弈）； 2 ）控制仓位，为后续配置保留空间； 3 ）调整结构，即便仍在科技内部，也
可以优先关注 AI 敞⼝低、或者估值低且更有议价能⼒的云⼚商和计算芯⽚等。
明年⼀季度是关键点： 1 ）美联储明显转向的契机预计要到明年初。10 ⽉强劲的⾮农强化了 12 ⽉
加息概率，明年初通胀在⾼基数带动下回落更确定；2 ）明年 1 ⽉披露的四季报。科技企业的
ROIC 可能继续承压，届时也更需要 AI 兑现新进展；3 ）美国新国会明年 1 ⽉ 3 ⽇就任。特朗普在
新国会到任前的政策与新当选议员不断发⾔可能会有持续扰动，市场需要消化，新国会就任后
可能逐步脱敏。所以，到那个时候，要不就是得 AI 有进展，要么就是得美联储紧缩预期退坡，
或许提供⽀撑或契机。
我们在《⺠主党 “ 横扫 ” 的资产含义》和《如何看待美股⼤跌？》中分别梳理了中期选举政策以及

市场动荡后的流动性扰动。本⽂中，我们进⼀步结合已经基本披露完毕的美股三季度业绩，从
基本⾯⻆度回答美股前景问题。
市场驱动⼒：盈利是市场的主导， AI 贡献六成增⻓；增速出现拐点
⾸先，盈利的强劲增⻓是美股的主要动⼒。年初⾄今，美股的主要指数涨幅均由盈利贡献，标
普 500 、道琼斯和纳斯达克指数分别上涨 8% 、 7% 和 6% ，其中动态盈利分别增⻓ 29% 、 17% 和
40% ，抵消了估值的收缩。三季度标普 500 盈利增⻓ 25% ，相⽐⼀季度和⼆季度的 32% 和 56% 有
所回落，但依然⾼于 2023-2025 年期间 12% 的季度盈利增速均值。
图表 5 ：今年以来的美股涨幅基本都是盈利贡献的，⽽⾮依靠估值扩张
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
其次， AI 相关板块贡献了六成的盈利增⻓。以通讯服务、信息技术和亚⻢逊估算 AI 板块的盈利情
况，三季度贡献了整体盈利增⻓的 62% ，低于⼆季度的 70% 。其中，信息技术贡献达 58% ，⽀
撑 AI 板块中的绝⼤部分增⻓，通讯服务则因⼦⾏业媒体娱乐增速⼤幅放缓，贡献明显⾛弱。这也

使得整体盈利增⻓系于 AI ⼀⾝，如果剔除 AI 板块的话，三季度盈利增速或将回落⾄ 15%。
图表 6 ：信息科技贡献本季度盈利增⻓的 58% ，通讯服务的贡献则由⼆季度的 24% 降⾄ 4%
